SOME SENSATIONAL GAMES t=.Nil RESULTS.

Owing to the miserable conditions last week- end, and intervening cup-ties causing fixtures to be withdrawn, there were no senior engage- ments in the town. The 'Latchford Club, not being able to fix up, had to have an idle day, while Crosfield's, owing to the wretched condi- tion of their ground, and the failure which attended the efforts of their secretary and com- mittee to obtain another: field, had to cancel their fixture with Stockton Heath.

The Adult School journeyed to Moore, where another even display was witnessed. Daresbury failed to find the net, whilst the visitors scored five times, and had they been steadier in front of goal they would probably have added more. The Wilderspool Brewery- met with a severe reverse on their own ground, to.- the tune of 12 goals to 3, Bank Quay Recs. being their victors.

In the Widnes and District Alliance the Stockton Heath Reserve entertained Ditton St. Mary's at West-avenue, and after a hard and well-contested game the points were divided, two goals each being the score. In the Widnes and District Medal Competi- tion the match between Longford United and Warrington Excelsior proved a great attraction. A strenuous and lard-fought first half yielded no points, and the second portion was just as keenly fought. The visitors managed to gain the verdict in their favour a few minutes before call of time,

Orford St. John's met Runcorn Heath at Long. lane, Orford,: and after, a well-contested game the home team ran out winners by' three goals to one.

Emmanuel Old Boys met Widnes ./‘ ibion in the same competition at Victoria Park, and wc:.e ,victorious by three goals to none. Orford Juniors made the journey to .13 unenrn and met the Weston team, and after a keen and well-fought game the score was one goal each. The Juniors were handicapped by hav- ing to, play three reserves in their team, and therefore should be able to win the replay on their ground. • Bank Quay Recs. and St. Barnabas' had a bye in the first round, and there should be six Warrington junior clubs in the next round. The draw takes place at Widnes on Tuesday next.

WARRINGTON AND DISTRICT LEAGUE. Orford Barracks v. Divex.

On the Soldiers' ground. Orford Barracks— Morrison; Tansley and Stevens ;. Moodie, Bir- kenhead and Topping; Roberts, Walker, 'Acton, Trafford and Spicer. Divex—Williams; Flute and Bullock; Rennie, Ashworth and Savage; Dronfield, Roberts, Curwen and Penfold. Referee, Mr.- T.. Pittam., , For thirtY, minutes the visitors held their own against their. More experienced opponents. ,Then the home team opened the score, and from now until the interval the Soldiers had the best of the game, and further goals were added. At half-time Orford Barracks were leading ,by four goals to nil.

On resuming, the Visitors were unable to keep the home forwards in check and the score was added to in a prolific manner. At the expiry of time the result was: Orford Barracks, 11 goals: Divex, nil. Roberts (three), Walker (two), Trafford (two), Acton (two), an Spicer (two), were the goal scorers.

aged by this early success, the visitors were hav- ing the best of the exchanges, Jefferson and Broadhurst being extended to clear their lines. The home forwards made a grand combined effott and Rigby equalised the scores with a splendid shot. From the centre-kick both teams made efforts to gain the lead, but no fur- ther score took place, and the interval arrived one goal each. On resuming, it was soon evident it would be a keen struggle for the lead, and the visitors were the first to become dangerous, but Jeffer- son drove them back with some fine returns, being ably assisted by Broadhurst. The home. half-backs now set their forwards going, and they paid a visit to the opposite end, Woodhead being a source of danger to the visitors' defence, troubling them with some fine centres,, and from a mistake by one of the visitors' backs a scramble took place in the goal-mouth, the ball being rushed through the goal. The home team was placed in front by this score. The game was now very fast The visitors were awarded a free-kick just outside the penalty area, this being well placed to their inside-right, who made no mistake with his shot and equalised the scores. From now to the close there was a hard §truggle for the lead, but no further score took place. Result: Stockton Heath Reserve, 2 goals; Ditton St. Mary's, 2 goals.

WIDNES MINOR MEDAL COMPETITION. Longford United were drawn against their neighbouring club, Warrington Excelsior, the match being played on the ground behind the Horse and Jockey Inn. The teams were : Longford United Holloway ; Mosley and Holden; Lea, H. Finch and Sherratt; A. Finch, W. Houghton, Blears, Helsby and Barton. Excelsior—Rigby; Boyd and Hughes; O'Neil, Spencer and Welsby ; Long, Walker, Leah, Hunt and Pritchard. Referee, Mr. S. C. Essenhigh. Owing to the local rivalry between the clubs, and the first time they had met in a competi- tion, there was a good number of supporters present. Blears kicked off and play began very even, and it was soon evident the game would be keenly fought, Longford were the first to get into a dangerous position, when Blears shot over the bar from an easy angle. The home team still had the best of the exchanges, but missed chances of scoring, and Boyd and Hughes eventually raised the siege. The visitors' for- wards carried play into the home quarters, and during an attack were awarded a penalty. Leah was entrusted with the kick, but 'Holloway made a clever save, and at halftime neither side had scored.

Play was fast and exciting. The visitors gradually gained the upper hand, and Walker opened-the score. This success made the players more keen, and each end was visited, the home side missing a good chance of scoring, by one of their forwards handling the ball. However, this did not dishearten them, and during an- other attack they obtained a corner. Barton placed the ball well in front of goal and it was headed past Rigby, and the team were now on level terms again. From the centre kick both sides now played hard for the lead, and many exciting incidents occurred, both teams missing chances through being too eager. When the second half had been in progress 35 minutes Long put the visitors in front again. The home team made strenuous efforts to get level, but Rigby, Boyd and Hughes wore all in fine form and prevented any further score, and time was called with the result: Warrington Excelsior, 2 goals; Longford United, 1.

Daresbury v. Adult School.*

These teams met on the ground of the former at Moore with the. following: —Darcsbury-- S. Ravenscraft; W. Forster and F. tle Prez; A. Cash, A. Ridyard and V. Ryder; W. Fleet, D. 'Adams, F. Forster, A. Ryder and 31. Forster. Adult •School—Welsby; Spilsbury and Garnett: Barton, Hunter and Leigh; Rowe, Dykes, McMutrie, Muckley and Banner, Referee, Mr r. J. Kite. The School, by good combination, forced the play to .the home. end, Ravenscroft mulling a shot in the opening stages. Dykes, with a grand opening, placed the ball wide of the posts. Receiving from the goal-kick, Fleet, Adams and H. Forster made a brief excursion to the visitors' end, but Hunter was alert, and, gaining possession, placed the leather to McMutrie. The latter sent out to Rowe on the extreme right, who beat Ryder and raced along the line, and centred at the right moment for Banner to rush in and score with a grand low- shot, leaving Ravenscroft helpless. At this point .the Adults were all over their opponents, and during an attack on the home goal the yisitors were awarded a penalty against W. Forster for a foul. The kick was taken by Hunter, and Ravenscroft, throwing himself full length across the goal, made a b,yillant save. The interval was called in favour ?If .e Adults by two goals to nil. The visitors made, tracks for the I :;me goal, and the game was confined chiefly around that citadel. 'Rowe added a couple of goals, while Muckley scored once. The home 'forwards could make very little impression on the visitors' defence, and Welsby had very little to do, only a few long shots coming his way. Result: Adult School, 5 goals; Daresbury, nil.

Wilderspool Brewery v. Bank Quay Recs. On Wilderspool Park: Wilderspool—Bostock; Dawson and Rutter; Brady, Barnes and Moores; Davenport, Hurst, Briscoe, Oakes and Hughes. Bank Quay Recs.—Bushell; Berry and Taylor ; Worrall, Lagar and Chesters ; Eccleston, Deakin, Bradshaw, Davies and Green. ' The visitors; after two minutes' play, were awarded a penalty against Rutter. Bradshaw was entrusted with the. kick, but Bostock saved, and, owing to an infringethent, the kick hid to be taken over again. Bradshaw made no mistake at the second attempt. The visitors were soon at the home -end again, and, owing to the home backs miskicking, Davies got through and scored a second goal, and directly after Chesters added to( the score. Bad play .on the part of Rutter let in Davies foi. a fourth .goal; Up to this point ,the visitors were by far the better team, and the home defence was being severely tested, Bostock saving several shots in a very smart manner, although Deakin beat him with a beauty. After these reverses the home team suddenly roused themselves and gave a surpris- ing display of. combination for three-parts of the field, and Oakes finished up with scoring for; the Brewery. From the centre-kick the .visitors rushed the home defence, Bradshaw finding the net, and two other goals were added by the latter and Davies, whilst Bristoe scored a second goal for the home team, the half-time score reading: Recs., 8 goals; Brewery, 2. The second portion opened more evenly and the home team looked like making amends, but their final shots were ;very, erratic, and from good clearances by the visitors' backs Green -got away on the left, and scored with a shot just inside the post. After this the visitors' :goal had a narrow 'escape from being captured, Bushell tipping the ball over the bar, conceding a corner. The flag-kick was well placed in the goal-mouth, and Moores put, the ball in the net, making the score three for the Brewers. The - visitors were awarded another- penalty, from which Bradshaw scored, and further scores were made by Berry and Eccleston. Result: Bank Quay Recs., 12 goals: Wilderspool Brewery, 3.

ASSOCIATION ITEMS.

Saturday was a miserable day for spectators and players in the " Soccer " code. Cup-ties and medal competitions are interfer- ing very much at present with the League fixtures.

There were five Warrington junior clubs en- gaged on Saturday in the Widnes Medal Coin- petition. There should be six eligible for the next round..

The Orford Barracks team enjoyed themselves at the expense of the Divex to the extent of 11 goals to nil. Five of their players contributed to the score. Roberts claiming three. Williams, Bullock and Penfold were the pick of the losing side. Daresbury were defeated on their own ground by the Adult School by s.goals to none. , Hunter is the mainspring of the ,Adult team. Spilsbury, Leigh, McMutrie and Rowe also did well.

Ravenscroft was good in goal for Daresbury. Ridyard, at centre-half, was the pick of the home team.

Bank Quay Recs. had a day out at Wilders- pool Park. They piled up 12 goals_ against the Brewers..

- - Teddy Webb, of Latchford; was plating again for Altrincham in the Lancashire \Combination. And so was Hinde, of Lymm. The Cheshire Amateur Cup tie between Stock- ton Heath and Latcliford takes place to-day. The Heathens are confident of winning. And so are Latchford players. The " Premier " says "Wait and see."

The Heathens have got a horseshoe for luck. Warrington and District League Committee are expecting a record entry for their Medal Coifipetifion. I The Adults have captured a fine half-back in Barton.

Dykes scored all the goals in the Adults' first three matches of the season. He has now lost his shooting boots. The meeting of Orford St. John's and Ex- celsior on Saturday at Long-lane will be a fight for the championship. • The John's intend to wipe out that defeat they sustained at the Excelsior's ground.

COLTNTY BOROUGH OF WARRINGTON. EDUCATION COMMITTEE. TO BUILDERS AND CONTRACTORS. The SITES and BUILDINGS COMMITTEE is prepared to receive - TENDERS for the ERECTION of a PUBLIC ELEMENTARY SCHOOL, to by known as the Oakwood Avenue Council Sclool. Bills of Quantities and Instructions to Builders and Contractors may be obtained on and after the 22nd December from the Educa- tion Office on payment of One Guinea, which will be returned upon the receipt of a bona-fide tender. The Tenders must be delivered at the Educa- tion Office in the envelope supplied (which must be securely sealed with wax) by not later than 12 o'clock noon on Friday, the 16th January, 1914. The Committee does not bind itself to accept the lowest or any Tender. J. MOORE MURRAY, -M.Sc., Secretary and Director. Edneotion Offic.e, Warrington, 10th December, 1913.

LOW- PRICED COKE. THE Warrington Corporation Gas Department , have decided to retail COKE in half-cwt. lots. Coke is the cheapest fuel, and if used instead Of coal would effect great economy and diminish Hinoke. , The Gas Department deliver Coke in cart loads 10 any customer, or in bags to several customers 'II the same street at small extra cost. COUNTY BOROUGH OF WARRINGTON. The Health Committee require the services of TWO LADY INSPECTORS, who shall act as Health Visitors, and, if required, as Inspectors of Midwives. Candidates must hold certificate for at least three years' training as Nurse in a General Hos- pital. and must also possess the certificate of the Central Midwives Board.. Preference will be given to candidates who have had nursing • experience among children, and hold the Health Visitor's Certificate, or the Sanitary Inspector's Certificate. Salary XBO per annum, rising by annual in- crements of £5 to a maximum of £lOO. „t 5 annual allowance for uniform. Applications to be made on forms to be obtained- from this office, together with copies of not more than three testimonials, to be sent in to the undersigned not later than Saturday, December 20th, 1913, and endorsed "Health Visitor." G, W. N. JOSEPH, Medical Officer of Health. Health Department, 78, Sankey-street, Warrington.

"The Biblical and Early Christian Idea of Pro- perty," and 'confirms what is said by , Scott Holland. He finds the conception expressed by David in addressing God on the occasion of the offerings of Israel for the building of the Temple: "Thine, 0 Lord, is the greatness, and the power. and the majesty: for all that is in the Heaven, and in the earth is thine; thine is the kingdom, 0 Lord, and thou art exalted as head above all. Both riches and honour come of thee, and thou rulest over all." Property rights in the Old Testament he claims to be "purely relative, derivative, • con- ditional, in the presence of God's Sovereign Lordship in all He has produced, ,and is pro- clueing, through the subordinate agencies of Nature and man." In other words, the general or naltional wel- fare is prior', to and determinative of that of the individual in the Divine order, and so by right. He cites such passages as: "Woe unto those who join house to house, who add field to field, till there is no more room, and ye are settled alone in the midst of the land." Property had no rights that were not relative to this great law of human life, that everything is to be judged .as having the sanction of God only so far as it subserves, or at least does not stand in the way of it. Thus every institution of society is to be regarded as liable to modification, -as in practice it fails to work in such a way as. to respect the sovereign rights of God in 'humanity • C. E. P. * PROPERTY: us DUTIES AND RIGHTS. By various writers, with an introduction by Bishop . Gore. London: Macmillan and Co., Ltd.

PUBLIC AMUSEMENTS. AN OPIUM DEN DREAM. Fine Programme at the Hippodrome. "A Dream in an Opium Den" is an excellent turn by the Six Ceylons, a troupe of alert and tricky acrobats and tumblers. The most daring tricks are performed in the most matter-of-fact way, whilst the antics of the diminutive mem- bers of the troupe cause roars of laughter. "The Vagabond" is an intensely interesting playlet, in which the pathetic and humorous aspects blend admirably. Mr. E. D. Nicholls ably plays the part of the vagabond. A mystifying turn is given by The Violas, who thoroughly bale the audience. and the illusions of Professor Nor- ton are cleverly carried out. Money Given Away. The millionaire entertainer, Mr. T. Elder Hearn. the marvellously clever illusionist, beads the bill for next week. He will give hundreds of postal orders away, at each performance. Tremendous interest is being centred on the great Continental Circus, which Mr. Ernest Wighton has arranged as the Christmas attrac- tion at Olympia. Nothing since the days of Barnum and Bailey ,so elaborate, costly and mag- nificent, has been seen in England. It is the greatest aggregation of Circus novelties the 'world can produce. Mr. Ernest Wighton has been very 'fortunate in securing the absolute cream of talent from all the big circuses of the world. HOW I RUBBED AWAY A STONE OF FAT FROM MY HIPS AND ABDOMEN IN TWO WEEKS TIME. For years I tried every possible means to hide the excessive fat on my hips and abdomen; it seemed as if it had accumulated on just the two places that it would be most noticeable. Everything that I tried failed to make me any thinner. But thanks to a lady .friend who had studied herboristerie, I learned the secret of a harmless plan which enabled -me to rub away with eakie 4 stone of useles§ fat hi •Otily two weeks. The plah ii ô simple and some of my friends seem so glad to know of it that I think there may be sister readers Of the Warrington " Examiner " who have perhaps been longing for this very advice. First, get from any good chemist 1 dram of quassia chips and 3 ounces of cirola bark extract. Take them home and after putting the quassia chips in a pan pour over it a teacupful of boiling water. When this has Stood for about a minute. strain through a cloth and add the cirola bark extract. Then pour the mixture into a bottle and apply it every night and morning with your hands for about ten minutes, using a circular movement. Each time you can almost see some of the fat melt away. At first I thought it must be a dream, but when I measured there' was no longer any doubt. I soon grew too Slender for my clothes, and had to have them taken in, but it was a pleasure to do it,. and I shall feel eternally grateful to the lady who showed me how easy it was to regain my youthful figure. E. L. A. WESLEYAN CAUSE THRIVING. Church Extension at Latchford. The Warrington Circuit quarterly meeting was held at Bold-street on Thursday. Tea was provided by the ladies of the Bold-street Church and the beautifully decorated tables aroused much admiration. The ladies were heartily thanked. The Chairman, the Rev. W. A. Beckett, was supported by all the circuit ministers and more than one hundred representatives from all parts of the circuit. The Chairman reported the mem- bership to show a very considerable increase on the corresponding quarter of last year. The stewards reported the finances to be in a very healthy state, showing a good balance in hand. The retiring circuit stewards, Mr. F. A. Har- ris and Dr. Ferguson, were heartily thanked for their services, and Mr. j. G. Withenshaw and Mr. R. 11. Meldrum were unanimously elected to fill their places. Mr. Murphy, of Penketh. was elected secretary of the quarterly meeting. The following appointments were made : Auditors, Messrs. Barlow and Pendlebury education secretary, Rev. E. Morgan; Guild secretary, Mr. K. Bishop. The local preachers were heartily thanked, many tributes being paid to the value of their services and their general efficiency. Mr. Wheatley „reported that a very successful class leaders' convention had been held, and Mr. Jackson had an equally good report as to the ,success of the aged -preachers' fund. The Rev. a Williamson reported that the Centenary Missionary fund collection in the circuit would, when all amounts were paid in I reach £550, and Mr. W. .Silcock said that dr usual missionary contributions were quite u to the average. The Chairman reported that land had, bee, secured adjoining the Wesleyan Church a Latchford for an extension of the premises, anN Iluilding operations were about to commence. 1 It was also, reported that Mrs. Dodds ha promised to give an organ to the Sankey Bridgr Wesleyan Church in Memory of her husban the late Mr. James Dodds. It was decided to accept the invitation of Bewsey-road Church to hold the next meet there.
